# Task
You will be given a **Requirement** and a **Class Diagram** in PlantUML. Your task is to annotate every operation listed in **Operations** with a specification.

An operation specification must describe the following sections about the operation:
1. Summary: the summary of functionality;
2. Algorithm: algorithm or pseudo code;
3. Related Features: a list of structural or behavior features within this class that shall be accessed;
4. Related Classes: a list of classes and data types defined in **Class Diagram** that shall be accessed, in the format of `Related Classes: ClsA, CLsB, ...` or `Related Classes: None`;
5. Input: the input parameters, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
6. Output: output value, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
7. Pre-condition: the condition that the system and the input must satisfy before the operation is executed;
8. Post-condition: the condition that the system and the output must satisfy after the operation is executed.

Finish this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Take the principle of modularization into account.
2. For each operation, determine its functional boundary based on the given requirement. Ensure high cohesion and low coupling.
3. If there is any unspecified details, such as undefined constants, rates, and ratios, then you must infer them.
4. Make sure that all requirements can be covered.

# Requirement
```text
Retail customers should be eligible for applying IPO. Customers who will make IPO Applications must share their name, surname, email address, and telephone number information with the related company.

When a customer applies IPO, he is required to upload legal allowance documentation. Each document has a name that identifies the allowance document. When the customer completes the application process successfully, the bank should send two information emails, one for the customer and another for the company. If the customer has many failed attempts, the bank should restrict the customer from the application process. If they would like to apply anyway, they should call the "call center" and request IPO application access.

Even though the customer completed the process successfully, the purchase is still being determined. Related companies always have a right to reject customers' applications. If a company rejects the customer, our system should inform the customer. Email content should include the customer's name, surname, email address, and telephone number, the applied company name, the number of shares that have been purchased, and the amount of money that has been paid.

Functional requirement:
1. Create an IPO application. A retail customer starts an IPO application by providing the target company, the number of shares greater than 0, the payment amount greater than 0, and a non-null allowance document. The system checks that the customer is currently eligible to apply and has no approved application for the same company name. The operation returns true when the application record is created successfully; otherwise, false.
2. Approve or reject an application. The bank can approve or reject only a pending application. Approval is allowed only when the related customer is still eligible to apply for IPO. When the bank approves an application, the system sends two information emails, one to the customer and one to the company. When the bank rejects an application, the system sends one rejection email to the customer, and the rejection email content includes the customer's name, surname, email address, telephone number, the company name, the number of shares, and the paid amount. The operation returns true on success; otherwise, false.
3. Retrieve a customer's application-count summary. The customer can view how many IPO applications have been completed in total. Only approved and rejected applications are counted. Pending applications and canceled applications are not counted. The operation returns 0 when the customer has no approved or rejected applications.
4. Query the total approved IPO application amount for a customer. The customer can view the sum of the paid amounts of all approved IPO applications. Pending and rejected applications are excluded from the total. The operation returns 0 when the customer has no approved applications.
5. Cancel a pending application. By providing a company name, a customer can cancel an application that is still pending. Approved and rejected applications cannot be canceled. The operation returns true when cancellation succeeds; otherwise, false.
```

# Class Diagram
```plantuml
@startuml

title ipo Model

class Application {
    amountOfMoney : double
    share : int
    status : ApplicationStatus
    allowance : Document
    company : Company
    customer : Customer
    emails : List<Email>
    approve() : boolean
    cancel() : boolean
    reject() : boolean
    sendRejectionEmail() : void
}
class Company {
    email : String
    name : String
}
class Customer {
    canApplyForIPO : boolean
    email : String
    name : String
    surname : String
    telephone : String
    applications : List<Application>
    cancelApplication(companyName : String) : boolean
    createApplication(company : Company, shares : int, amount : double, doc : Document) : boolean
    getApplicationCount() : int
    getApprovedTotalAmount() : double
    isEligibleForIPO() : boolean
}
class Document {
    name : String
}
class Email {
    content : String
    receiver : String
    createEmailContent(company : Company, shares : int, customer : Customer, amount : double) : String
}
enum ApplicationStatus {
    PENDING
    APPROVAL
    REJECTED
}

Application --> Document : allowance
Application --> Company : company
Application --> Customer : customer  'opposite: applications
Application *-- "*" Email : emails
Customer *-- "*" Application : applications  'opposite: customer
@enduml
```

Returns the sum of paid amounts for all approved IPO applications of this customer.
Pending and rejected applications are excluded.
Algorithm:
1. Initialize total to 0.0.
2. Iterate through applications.
3. For each application, if status is APPROVAL, add application.amountOfMoney to total.
4. Return total.
Related Features:
- applications : List<Application>
- getApprovedTotalAmount() : double
- amountOfMoney : double
Related Classes: Application, ApplicationStatus
Input:
- No explicit input parameters.
Output:
- double: sum of paid amounts of approved applications.
- Returns 0.0 when there are no approved applications.
Pre-condition:
- The customer object must exist.
- The applications collection may be empty.
Post-condition:
- The returned value equals the sum of amountOfMoney for applications with status APPROVAL.
- The customer state is not modified., org.eclipse.emf.ecore.impl.EOperationImpl@100ad67e (name: reject) (ordered: true, unique: true, lowerBound: 0, upperBound: 1)=Summary:
Rejects a pending IPO application.
Only a pending application can be rejected.
When rejected, the application status changes to REJECTED and a rejection email should be prepared/sent through the rejection-email flow.
Algorithm:
1. Check whether status is PENDING.
2. If not PENDING, return false.
3. Set status to REJECTED.
4. Prepare the rejection email content using the linked customer, company, shares, and paid amount.
5. Add or trigger the rejection email in emails.
6. Return true.

- status : ApplicationStatus
- customer : Customer
- company : Company
- share : int
- amountOfMoney : double

- sendRejectionEmail() : void

- No explicit input parameters.
- Uses the current Application instance state.
Output:
- boolean: true if rejection succeeds, otherwise false.
Pre-condition:
- The application must exist and be linked to a customer and a company.
- The application must currently be in PENDING status.
Post-condition:
- If the result is true, the application status is REJECTED.
- A rejection email is generated/sent for the customer.
- If the result is false, the application state remains unchanged., org.eclipse.emf.ecore.impl.EOperationImpl@62aeddc8 (name: sendRejectionEmail) (ordered: true, unique: true, lowerBound: 0, upperBound: 1)=Summary:
Creates and/or sends the rejection email for the current application.
The rejection email must include the customer's name, surname, email address, telephone number, the company name, the number of shares, and the paid amount.
Algorithm:
1. Create an Email object if one does not already exist for this rejection event.
2. Set the receiver to the customer's email address.
3. Build the content using customer, company, share, and amount information.
4. Store the email in emails and/or trigger delivery.
5. Return void.
Related Features:
- emails : List<Email>
- customer : Customer
- company : Company
- share : int
- amountOfMoney : double
- sendRejectionEmail() : void
Related Classes: Email, Customer, Company

- No explicit input parameters.
- Uses the current Application instance state.
Output:
- void.
- Side effect: a rejection email is prepared and associated with the application.
Pre-condition:
- The application must have a linked customer and company.
- The application should be in REJECTED status or in the process of being rejected.

# Task
You will be given a **Requirement**, a **Class Diagram** in PlantUML, and a list of operation specifications. Your task is to correct the specifications that are inconsistent with the requirement.

An operation specification must describe the following sections about the operation:
1. Summary: the summary of functionality;
2. Algorithm: algorithm or pseudo code;
3. Related Features: a list of structural or behavior features within this class that shall be accessed;
4. Related Classes: a list of classes and data types defined in **Class Diagram** that shall be accessed, in the format of `Related Classes: ClsA, CLsB, ...` or `Related Classes: None`;
5. Input: the input parameters, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
6. Output: output value, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
7. Pre-condition: the condition that the system and the input must satisfy before the operation is executed;
8. Post-condition: the condition that the system and the output must satisfy after the operation is executed.

Finish this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Read the requirement and the class diagram.
2. For each operation specification, determine whether it is complete and consistent with the requirement.
3. If there is any error, correct the erroneous specification.
4. Follow the specification format. Output changed specifications only; OMIT unchanged specifications.

# Requirement
```text
Retail customers should be eligible for applying IPO. Customers who will make IPO Applications must share their name, surname, email address, and telephone number information with the related company.

When a customer applies IPO, he is required to upload legal allowance documentation. Each document has a name that identifies the allowance document. When the customer completes the application process successfully, the bank should send two information emails, one for the customer and another for the company. If the customer has many failed attempts, the bank should restrict the customer from the application process. If they would like to apply anyway, they should call the "call center" and request IPO application access.

Even though the customer completed the process successfully, the purchase is still being determined. Related companies always have a right to reject customers' applications. If a company rejects the customer, our system should inform the customer. Email content should include the customer's name, surname, email address, and telephone number, the applied company name, the number of shares that have been purchased, and the amount of money that has been paid.

Functional requirement:
1. Create an IPO application. A retail customer starts an IPO application by providing the target company, the number of shares greater than 0, the payment amount greater than 0, and a non-null allowance document. The system checks that the customer is currently eligible to apply and has no approved application for the same company name. The operation returns true when the application record is created successfully; otherwise, false.
2. Approve or reject an application. The bank can approve or reject only a pending application. Approval is allowed only when the related customer is still eligible to apply for IPO. When the bank approves an application, the system sends two information emails, one to the customer and one to the company. When the bank rejects an application, the system sends one rejection email to the customer, and the rejection email content includes the customer's name, surname, email address, telephone number, the company name, the number of shares, and the paid amount. The operation returns true on success; otherwise, false.
3. Retrieve a customer's application-count summary. The customer can view how many IPO applications have been completed in total. Only approved and rejected applications are counted. Pending applications and canceled applications are not counted. The operation returns 0 when the customer has no approved or rejected applications.
4. Query the total approved IPO application amount for a customer. The customer can view the sum of the paid amounts of all approved IPO applications. Pending and rejected applications are excluded from the total. The operation returns 0 when the customer has no approved applications.
5. Cancel a pending application. By providing a company name, a customer can cancel an application that is still pending. Approved and rejected applications cannot be canceled. The operation returns true when cancellation succeeds; otherwise, false.
```

    name : String
}

enum ApplicationStatus {
    PENDING
    APPROVAL
    REJECTED
}

Application --> Document : allowance
Application --> Company : company
Application --> Customer : customer  'opposite: applications

Customer *-- "*" Application : applications  'opposite: customer
@enduml

# Operation Specifications
Customer::getApprovedTotalAmount
Summary:
Returns the sum of paid amounts for all approved IPO applications of this customer.
Pending and rejected applications are excluded.
Algorithm:
1. Initialize total to 0.0.
2. Iterate through applications.
3. For each application, if status is APPROVAL, add application.amountOfMoney to total.
4. Return total.
Related Features:
- applications : List<Application>
- getApprovedTotalAmount() : double
- amountOfMoney : double
Related Classes: Application, ApplicationStatus
Input:
- No explicit input parameters.
Output:
- double: sum of paid amounts of approved applications.
- Returns 0.0 when there are no approved applications.
Pre-condition:
- The customer object must exist.
- The applications collection may be empty.
Post-condition:
- The returned value equals the sum of amountOfMoney for applications with status APPROVAL.
- The customer state is not modified.

Customer::isEligibleForIPO
Summary:
Determines whether the customer is currently eligible to apply for IPO.
Eligibility is controlled by the canApplyForIPO flag.
If the customer has many failed attempts, this flag is expected to be false until call-center access is granted.
Algorithm:
1. Read canApplyForIPO.
2. Return its value.
Related Features:

- The customer state is not modified.

Customer::createApplication
Summary:
Creates a new IPO application for a retail customer.
The customer must provide the target company, a share count greater than 0, a payment amount greater than 0, and a non-null allowance document.
The customer must be currently eligible to apply and must not already have an approved application for the same company name.
Returns true if the application record is created successfully; otherwise false.
Algorithm:
1. Validate inputs: company is non-null, shares > 0, amount > 0, doc is non-null.
2. Check isEligibleForIPO().
3. Check whether there is already an approved application for the same company.name in applications.
4. If any check fails, return false.
5. Create a new Application instance.
6. Set customer, company, share, amountOfMoney, allowance, and initial status PENDING.
7. Add the application to applications.
8. Return true.
Related Features:
- canApplyForIPO : boolean
- email : String
- name : String
- surname : String
- telephone : String
- applications : List<Application>
- createApplication(company : Company, shares : int, amount : double, doc : Document) : boolean
- isEligibleForIPO() : boolean
Related Classes: Application, Company, Document, ApplicationStatus
Input:
- company : Company
  - Target company for IPO.
  - Must not be null.
- shares : int
  - Number of shares to apply for.
  - Must be greater than 0.
- amount : double
  - Payment amount.
  - Must be greater than 0.
- doc : Document
  - Legal allowance documentation.
  - Must not be null.
Output:
- boolean: true if the application is created and stored successfully; otherwise false.
Pre-condition:
- Customer must be a retail customer in the IPO context.
- Customer must be eligible to apply for IPO.
- The provided company, shares, amount, and document must satisfy the validation rules.
- No approved application for the same company name may already exist for this customer.
Post-condition:
- If true, a new pending application is added to the customer's applications list.
- The new application contains the provided company, share count, amount, and allowance document.
- If false, no new application is created.

You will be given a **Requirement**, a **Class Diagram** in PlantUML, and a list of operation specifications. Your task is to correct the specifications that are inconsistent with the requirement.

# Requirement
```text
Retail customers should be eligible for applying IPO. Customers who will make IPO Applications must share their name, surname, email address, and telephone number information with the related company.

When a customer applies IPO, he is required to upload legal allowance documentation. Each document has a name that identifies the allowance document. When the customer completes the application process successfully, the bank should send two information emails, one for the customer and another for the company. If the customer has many failed attempts, the bank should restrict the customer from the application process. If they would like to apply anyway, they should call the "call center" and request IPO application access.

Even though the customer completed the process successfully, the purchase is still being determined. Related companies always have a right to reject customers' applications. If a company rejects the customer, our system should inform the customer. Email content should include the customer's name, surname, email address, and telephone number, the applied company name, the number of shares that have been purchased, and the amount of money that has been paid.

Functional requirement:
1. Create an IPO application. A retail customer starts an IPO application by providing the target company, the number of shares greater than 0, the payment amount greater than 0, and a non-null allowance document. The system checks that the customer is currently eligible to apply and has no approved application for the same company name. The operation returns true when the application record is created successfully; otherwise, false.
2. Approve or reject an application. The bank can approve or reject only a pending application. Approval is allowed only when the related customer is still eligible to apply for IPO. When the bank approves an application, the system sends two information emails, one to the customer and one to the company. When the bank rejects an application, the system sends one rejection email to the customer, and the rejection email content includes the customer's name, surname, email address, telephone number, the company name, the number of shares, and the paid amount. The operation returns true on success; otherwise, false.
3. Retrieve a customer's application-count summary. The customer can view how many IPO applications have been completed in total. Only approved and rejected applications are counted. Pending applications and canceled applications are not counted. The operation returns 0 when the customer has no approved or rejected applications.
4. Query the total approved IPO application amount for a customer. The customer can view the sum of the paid amounts of all approved IPO applications. Pending and rejected applications are excluded from the total. The operation returns 0 when the customer has no approved applications.
5. Cancel a pending application. By providing a company name, a customer can cancel an application that is still pending. Approved and rejected applications cannot be canceled. The operation returns true when cancellation succeeds; otherwise, false.
```

# Operation Specifications
Application::reject
Summary:
Rejects a pending IPO application.
Only a pending application can be rejected.
When rejected, the application status changes to REJECTED and a rejection email should be prepared/sent through the rejection-email flow.
Algorithm:
1. Check whether status is PENDING.
2. If not PENDING, return false.
3. Set status to REJECTED.
4. Prepare the rejection email content using the linked customer, company, shares, and paid amount.
5. Add or trigger the rejection email in emails.
6. Return true.
Related Features:
- status : ApplicationStatus
- customer : Customer
- company : Company
- share : int
- amountOfMoney : double
- emails : List<Email>
- reject() : boolean
- sendRejectionEmail() : void
Related Classes: ApplicationStatus, Customer, Company, Email
Input:
- No explicit input parameters.
- Uses the current Application instance state.
Output:
- boolean: true if rejection succeeds, otherwise false.
Pre-condition:
- The application must exist and be linked to a customer and a company.
- The application must currently be in PENDING status.
Post-condition:
- If the result is true, the application status is REJECTED.
- A rejection email is generated/sent for the customer.
- If the result is false, the application state remains unchanged.

Application::sendRejectionEmail
Summary:
Creates and/or sends the rejection email for the current application.
The rejection email must include the customer's name, surname, email address, telephone number, the company name, the number of shares, and the paid amount.
Algorithm:
1. Create an Email object if one does not already exist for this rejection event.
2. Set the receiver to the customer's email address.
3. Build the content using customer, company, share, and amount information.
4. Store the email in emails and/or trigger delivery.
5. Return void.
Related Features:
- emails : List<Email>
- customer : Customer
- company : Company
- share : int
- amountOfMoney : double
- sendRejectionEmail() : void
Related Classes: Email, Customer, Company
Input:
- No explicit input parameters.
- Uses the current Application instance state.
Output:
- void.
- Side effect: a rejection email is prepared and associated with the application.
Pre-condition:
- The application must have a linked customer and company.
- The application should be in REJECTED status or in the process of being rejected.
Post-condition:
- The rejection email content exists and includes all required customer and application data.
- The email receiver is the customer email address.

Application::approve
Summary:
Approves a pending IPO application if and only if the application is currently in PENDING status and the related customer is still eligible to apply for IPO.
On success, the application status becomes APPROVAL and the approval process may trigger information emails to the customer and the company at a higher-level flow.
Algorithm:
1. Check whether this application's status is PENDING.
2. Check whether the related customer satisfies isEligibleForIPO().
3. If either check fails, return false.
4. Set status to APPROVAL.
5. Return true.
Related Features:
- status : ApplicationStatus
- customer : Customer
- approve() : boolean
- emails : List<Email>
Related Classes: ApplicationStatus, Customer, Email
Input:
- No explicit input parameters.
- Uses the current Application instance state, especially status and linked customer.
Output:
- boolean: true if approval succeeds, otherwise false.
Pre-condition:
- The application object must be initialized with a linked customer.
- The application must belong to the pending workflow before approval is attempted.
- The system must be able to evaluate the customer eligibility for IPO.
Post-condition:
- If the result is true, the application status is APPROVAL.
- If the result is false, the application state remains unchanged.
- No non-approved application is marked as approved.

Application::cancel
Summary:
Cancels a pending IPO application.
Only applications in PENDING status can be canceled; approved or rejected applications cannot be canceled.
Algorithm:
1. Check whether status is PENDING.
2. If not PENDING, return false.
3. Set status to canceled state.
   Note: Since the enum does not define a CANCELED value, infer that the implementation must represent cancellation by removing the application from the customer's active application list or by using an internal canceled marker outside the provided enum.
4. Return true.
Related Features:
- status : ApplicationStatus
- cancel() : boolean
- customer : Customer
Related Classes: ApplicationStatus, Customer
Input:
- No explicit input parameters.
- Uses the current Application instance state.
Output:
- boolean: true if cancellation succeeds, otherwise false.
Pre-condition:
- The application must exist and be linked to a customer.
- The application must currently be in PENDING status.
Post-condition:
- If the result is true, the application is no longer considered active/pending.
- Approved or rejected applications remain unchanged and cannot be canceled.

# Operation Specifications
Email::createEmailContent
Summary:
Builds the email body content for IPO application information or rejection notifications.
The content must include the customer's name, surname, email address, telephone number, the company name, the number of shares, and the paid amount.
Algorithm:
1. Read customer fields: name, surname, email, telephone.
2. Read company.name, shares, and amount.
3. Concatenate or format these values into a human-readable message.
4. Return the resulting string.

- content : String
- receiver : String
- createEmailContent(company : Company, shares : int, customer : Customer, amount : double) : String

  - Must be greater than 0.

  - Must be greater than 0.

- shares and amount must be valid positive values.

You will be given a **Requirement**, a **Class Diagram** in PlantUML, and a list of operation specifications. Your task is to correct the specifications that are inconsistent with the requirement.

# Requirement
```text
Retail customers should be eligible for applying IPO. Customers who will make IPO Applications must share their name, surname, email address, and telephone number information with the related company.

When a customer applies IPO, he is required to upload legal allowance documentation. Each document has a name that identifies the allowance document. When the customer completes the application process successfully, the bank should send two information emails, one for the customer and another for the company. If the customer has many failed attempts, the bank should restrict the customer from the application process. If they would like to apply anyway, they should call the "call center" and request IPO application access.

Even though the customer completed the process successfully, the purchase is still being determined. Related companies always have a right to reject customers' applications. If a company rejects the customer, our system should inform the customer. Email content should include the customer's name, surname, email address, and telephone number, the applied company name, the number of shares that have been purchased, and the amount of money that has been paid.

Functional requirement:
1. Create an IPO application. A retail customer starts an IPO application by providing the target company, the number of shares greater than 0, the payment amount greater than 0, and a non-null allowance document. The system checks that the customer is currently eligible to apply and has no approved application for the same company name. The operation returns true when the application record is created successfully; otherwise, false.
2. Approve or reject an application. The bank can approve or reject only a pending application. Approval is allowed only when the related customer is still eligible to apply for IPO. When the bank approves an application, the system sends two information emails, one to the customer and one to the company. When the bank rejects an application, the system sends one rejection email to the customer, and the rejection email content includes the customer's name, surname, email address, telephone number, the company name, the number of shares, and the paid amount. The operation returns true on success; otherwise, false.
3. Retrieve a customer's application-count summary. The customer can view how many IPO applications have been completed in total. Only approved and rejected applications are counted. Pending applications and canceled applications are not counted. The operation returns 0 when the customer has no approved or rejected applications.
4. Query the total approved IPO application amount for a customer. The customer can view the sum of the paid amounts of all approved IPO applications. Pending and rejected applications are excluded from the total. The operation returns 0 when the customer has no approved applications.
5. Cancel a pending application. By providing a company name, a customer can cancel an application that is still pending. Approved and rejected applications cannot be canceled. The operation returns true when cancellation succeeds; otherwise, false.
```

Complete this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Compute context information
  1.1 Consider the related classes in the Javadocs.
  1.2 Locate these files in your project.
  1.3 Generate the class summarization.
2. A method is unimplemented if its body contains `// TODO: Please complete this Java method based on the docstring`.
3. For every unimplemented method, 
  3.1 You must read the docstring of this method. The fragment between `<!-- begin-model-doc -->` and `<!-- end-model-doc -->` contains the method specification;
  3.2 Generate your code strictly according to the algorithm; Be aware of the EMF code style and APIs;
  3.3 You should not change the method signature;
  3.4 After generation, replace the current body with the one you generate;
  3.5 Keep and update the docstring by changing `@generated` to `@generated NOT`.
4. Check the import list. Add missing imports if needed.
5. Do not change other part.
6. Be aware of the method signatures, particularly, the input and output types, provided in the context.

## EMF Coding Style
- When possible, call getter/setter methods to access field values. Do not read/write raw fields derived from the class diagram.
- The getter of a boolean attribute `bv` looks like `isBv()`, e.g., `isIsTrue()` for `isTrue`.
- When possibe, use the creation factory for object creation.
- To create an EList instance, import and use `org.eclipse.emf.common.util.BasicEList`.

Complete this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Compute context information
  1.1 Consider the related classes in the Javadocs.
  1.2 Locate these files in your project.
  1.3 Generate the class summarization.
2. A method is unimplemented if its body contains `// TODO: Please complete this Java method based on the docstring`.
3. For every unimplemented method, 
  3.1 You must read the docstring of this method. The fragment between `<!-- begin-model-doc -->` and `<!-- end-model-doc -->` contains the method specification;
  3.2 Generate your code strictly according to the algorithm; Be aware of the EMF code style and APIs;
  3.3 You should not change the method signature;
  3.4 After generation, replace the current body with the one you generate;
  3.5 Keep and update the docstring by changing `@generated` to `@generated NOT`.
4. Check the import list. Add missing imports if needed.
5. Do not change other part.
6. Be aware of the method signatures, particularly, the input and output types, provided in the context.

## EMF Coding Style
- When possible, call getter/setter methods to access field values. Do not read/write raw fields derived from the class diagram.
- The getter of a boolean attribute `bv` looks like `isBv()`, e.g., `isIsTrue()` for `isTrue`.
- When possibe, use the creation factory for object creation.
- To create an EList instance, import and use `org.eclipse.emf.common.util.BasicEList`.

   * <!-- begin-user-doc -->
   * <!-- end-user-doc -->
   * <!-- begin-model-doc -->
   * Summary:
   * Creates a new IPO application for a retail customer.
   * The customer must provide the target company, a share count greater than 0, a payment amount greater than 0, and a non-null allowance document.
   * The customer must be currently eligible to apply and must not already have an approved application for the same company name.
   * Returns true if the application record is created successfully; otherwise false.
   * Algorithm:
   * 1. Validate inputs: company is non-null, shares > 0, amount > 0, doc is non-null.
   * 2. Check isEligibleForIPO().
   * 3. Check whether there is already an approved application for the same company.name in applications.
   * 4. If any check fails, return false.
   * 5. Create a new Application instance.
   * 6. Set customer, company, share, amountOfMoney, allowance, and initial status PENDING.
   * 7. Add the application to applications.
   * 8. Return true.
   * Related Features:

   * - email : String
   * - name : String
   * - surname : String
   * - telephone : String

   * - isEligibleForIPO() : boolean
   * Related Classes: Application, Company, Document, ApplicationStatus
   * Input:

   *   - Target company for IPO.
   *   - Must not be null.
   * - shares : int
   *   - Number of shares to apply for.
   *   - Must be greater than 0.
   * - amount : double
   *   - Payment amount.
   *   - Must be greater than 0.
   * - doc : Document
   *   - Legal allowance documentation.
   *   - Must not be null.
   * Output:
   * - boolean: true if the application is created and stored successfully; otherwise false.
   * Pre-condition:
   * - Customer must be a retail customer in the IPO context.
   * - Customer must be eligible to apply for IPO.
   * - The provided company, shares, amount, and document must satisfy the validation rules.
   * - No approved application for the same company name may already exist for this customer.
   * Post-condition:
   * - If true, a new pending application is added to the customer's applications list.
   * - The new application contains the provided company, share count, amount, and allowance document.
   * - If false, no new application is created.

   * @model annotation="http://www.ustb.edu.cn/sei/mde/EnhancedCodeGen generated='true'"
   * @generated NOT

  public boolean createApplication(final Company company, final int shares, final double amount, final Document doc)
  {
    if (company == null || shares <= 0 || amount <= 0.0 || doc == null)
    {
      return false;
    }

    if (!isEligibleForIPO())
    {
      return false;
    }

    for (Application application : getApplications())
    {
      if (application.getCompany() != null
          && company.getName() != null
          && company.getName().equals(application.getCompany().getName())
          && application.getStatus() == ApplicationStatus.APPROVAL)
      {
        return false;
      }
    }

    Application application = IpoFactory.eINSTANCE.createApplication();
    application.setCustomer(this);
    application.setCompany(company);
    application.setShare(shares);
    application.setAmountOfMoney(amount);
    application.setAllowance(doc);
    application.setStatus(ApplicationStatus.PENDING);
    getApplications().add(application);
    return true;
    
  }

  /**
   * <!-- begin-user-doc -->
   * <!-- end-user-doc -->
   * <!-- begin-model-doc -->
   * Summary:
   * Cancels a pending application of this customer for the specified company name.
   * Only applications that are still pending can be canceled.
   * Algorithm:
   * 1. Search the customer's applications for an application whose company.name matches companyName.
   * 2. If no such application exists, return false.
   * 3. If the matched application is not PENDING, return false.
   * 4. Call application.cancel().
   * 5. If cancellation succeeds, remove or mark the application as inactive in the customer's application collection.
   * 6. Return the cancellation result.
   * Related Features:
   * - applications : List<Application>
   * - cancelApplication(companyName : String) : boolean
   * Related Classes: Application, Company
   * Input:
   * - companyName : String
   *   - The target company name.
   *   - Must be a non-null, non-empty string.
   *   - Matching is based on company name equality.
   * Output:
   * - boolean: true if a pending application for the given company was canceled successfully; otherwise false.
   * Pre-condition:
   * - The customer object must exist.
   * - The companyName must be provided.
   * - The customer must have an application matching the company name to cancel.
   * Post-condition:
   * - If true, the matched application is no longer pending/active.
   * - The customer's application collection reflects the cancellation.
   * <!-- end-model-doc -->
   * @model annotation="http://www.ustb.edu.cn/sei/mde/EnhancedCodeGen generated='true'"
   * @generated NOT
   */
  public boolean cancelApplication(final String companyName)
  {
    if (companyName == null || companyName.length() == 0)
    {
      return false;
    }

    for (Application application : getApplications())
    {
      if (application.getCompany() != null
          && companyName.equals(application.getCompany().getName()))
      {
        if (application.getStatus() != ApplicationStatus.PENDING)
        {
          return false;
        }
        boolean canceled = application.cancel();
        if (canceled)
        {
          getApplications().remove(application);
        }
        return canceled;
      }
    }
    return false;
    
  }

        return EMAIL_EDEFAULT == null ? email != null : !EMAIL_EDEFAULT.equals(email);
      case IpoPackage.CUSTOMER__TELEPHONE:
        return TELEPHONE_EDEFAULT == null ? telephone != null : !TELEPHONE_EDEFAULT.equals(telephone);
      case IpoPackage.CUSTOMER__CAN_APPLY_FOR_IPO:
        return canApplyForIPO != CAN_APPLY_FOR_IPO_EDEFAULT;
      case IpoPackage.CUSTOMER__APPLICATIONS:
        return applications != null && !applications.isEmpty();
    }
    return super.eIsSet(featureID);
  }

  @Override
  public String toString()
  {
    if (eIsProxy()) return super.toString();

    StringBuilder result = new StringBuilder(super.toString());
    result.append(" (name: ");
    result.append(name);
    result.append(", surname: ");
    result.append(surname);
    result.append(", email: ");
    result.append(email);
    result.append(", telephone: ");
    result.append(telephone);
    result.append(", canApplyForIPO: ");
    result.append(canApplyForIPO);
    result.append(')');
    return result.toString();
  }

Complete this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Compute context information

  1.2 Locate these files in your project.
  1.3 Generate the class summarization.
2. A method is unimplemented if its body contains `// TODO: Please complete this Java method based on the docstring`.
3. For every unimplemented method, 
  3.1 You must read the docstring of this method. The fragment between `<!-- begin-model-doc -->` and `<!-- end-model-doc -->` contains the method specification;
  3.2 Generate your code strictly according to the algorithm; Be aware of the EMF code style and APIs;
  3.3 You should not change the method signature;
  3.4 After generation, replace the current body with the one you generate;
  3.5 Keep and update the docstring by changing `@generated` to `@generated NOT`.
4. Check the import list. Add missing imports if needed.
5. Do not change other part.
6. Be aware of the method signatures, particularly, the input and output types, provided in the context.

## EMF Coding Style
- When possible, call getter/setter methods to access field values. Do not read/write raw fields derived from the class diagram.
- The getter of a boolean attribute `bv` looks like `isBv()`, e.g., `isIsTrue()` for `isTrue`.
- When possibe, use the creation factory for object creation.
- To create an EList instance, import and use `org.eclipse.emf.common.util.BasicEList`.

Complete this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Compute context information
  1.1 Consider the related classes in the Javadocs.
  1.2 Locate these files in your project.
  1.3 Generate the class summarization.
2. A method is unimplemented if its body contains `// TODO: Please complete this Java method based on the docstring`.
3. For every unimplemented method, 
  3.1 You must read the docstring of this method. The fragment between `<!-- begin-model-doc -->` and `<!-- end-model-doc -->` contains the method specification;
  3.2 Generate your code strictly according to the algorithm; Be aware of the EMF code style and APIs;
  3.3 You should not change the method signature;
  3.4 After generation, replace the current body with the one you generate;
  3.5 Keep and update the docstring by changing `@generated` to `@generated NOT`.
4. Check the import list. Add missing imports if needed.
5. Do not change other part.
6. Be aware of the method signatures, particularly, the input and output types, provided in the context.

## EMF Coding Style
- When possible, call getter/setter methods to access field values. Do not read/write raw fields derived from the class diagram.
- The getter of a boolean attribute `bv` looks like `isBv()`, e.g., `isIsTrue()` for `isTrue`.
- When possibe, use the creation factory for object creation.
- To create an EList instance, import and use `org.eclipse.emf.common.util.BasicEList`.

  public boolean approve()
